### Learning-Time Encoding Shapes Unlearning in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15076v1)
- **Authors**: Ruihan Wu, Konstantin Garov, Kamalika Chaudhuri
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in the real world, the ability to ``unlearn'', or remove specific pieces of knowledge post hoc, has become essential for a variety of reasons ranging from privacy regulations to correcting outdated or harmful content. Prior work has proposed unlearning benchmarks and algorithms, and has typically assumed that the training process and the target model are fixed. In this work, we empirically investigate how learning-time choices in knowledge encoding impact the effectiveness of unlearning factual knowledge. Our experiments reveal two key findings: (1) learning with paraphrased descriptions improves unlearning performance and (2) unlearning individual piece of knowledge from a chunk of text is challenging. Our results suggest that learning-time knowledge encoding may play a central role in enabling reliable post-hoc unlearning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores how the way knowledge is encoded during the training of Large Language Models (LLMs) impacts the effectiveness of subsequent unlearning.  It examines two key factors: (1) using paraphrased descriptions during fine-tuning and (2) the entanglement of knowledge within text chunks. The authors conduct controlled experiments on augmented versions of the Eval-DU and TOFU datasets, finetuning various LLMs (Llama2, Llama3, Gemma) and then applying gradient ascent and task vector methods for unlearning.  The results demonstrate that learning with paraphrased descriptions enhances unlearning performance, and that unlearning is significantly more challenging when target knowledge is intertwined with retained knowledge in the same text chunks. The paper proposes strategies such as paraphrasing and separating (structuring training data to avoid knowledge entanglement) to improve post-hoc unlearning efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea – investigating the *impact of learning-time encoding choices on post-hoc unlearning* – is a valuable perspective that has received relatively less attention. Prior works primarily focus on algorithm development and benchmark design, without closely examining the influence of data representation. The decomposition of the problem into paraphrasing and text chunking aspects is also novel and provides actionable insights.

*   **Significance:** The findings have direct implications for how LLMs are trained if unlearning is anticipated. If paraphrasing is used more often, better unlearning could be done post hoc.  The observation that knowledge entanglement hinders unlearning is also significant and highlights the importance of careful data structuring for privacy and compliance. The suggested strategies (paraphrasing and separation) offer practical guidance to those building and deploying LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem and controlled experiments:** The paper rigorously controls the knowledge space by using synthetic biographies, enabling clear isolation of the effects of different encoding methods.
    *   **Empirical validation:** The experiments are comprehensive, covering multiple datasets, model architectures, and unlearning algorithms, which strengthens the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Clear presentation and actionable insights:** The paper clearly articulates the problem, methodology, results, and practical implications, making it accessible and useful for practitioners.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Fine-tuning focus:** The study focuses on unlearning from fine-tuned models.  While this is a common scenario, generalizability to the pre-training phase, although partially addressed, remains somewhat limited due to the lack of visibility into pretraining data and computational costs.  Further studies on the influence on pretraining phase are needed.
    *   **Synthetic data:** While the use of synthetic data allows precise control, it raises questions about the applicability of the findings to real-world datasets with more complex and noisy knowledge representations. While useful and valuable for the paper, additional research with more complex data representation is needed.
    *   **Limited exploration of other encoding methods:** The paper mainly explores paraphrasing and text chunking. A broader range of encoding methods, such as different levels of abstraction or explicit knowledge graph integration, could provide even richer insights.
    *   **Limited analysis of utility preservation:** The paper primarily focuses on the unlearning vs. retain tradeoff. An investigation into the impact of different encoding strategies on preserving other aspects of model utility is needed.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could influence the development of new training methodologies for LLMs that are more amenable to post-hoc unlearning. It may also motivate further research into the interplay between knowledge representation and unlearning techniques.

*   **Justification of score:**  The paper makes a meaningful contribution by highlighting the previously under-explored role of learning-time knowledge encoding in unlearning effectiveness. While limited to fine-tuning and synthetic data, the rigorous experimentation and practical strategies proposed make it a valuable contribution to the field. It provides a useful and novel focus, and its impact is likely to grow as unlearning becomes more crucial for large language models.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enhancement Report Approval Prediction: A Comparative Study of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15098v1)
- **Authors**: Haosheng Zuo, Feifei Niu, Chuanyi Li
- **Abstract**: Enhancement reports (ERs) serve as a critical communication channel between users and developers, capturing valuable suggestions for software improvement. However, manually processing these reports is resource-intensive, leading to delays and potential loss of valuable insights. To address this challenge, enhancement report approval prediction (ERAP) has emerged as a research focus, leveraging machine learning techniques to automate decision-making. While traditional approaches have employed feature-based classifiers and deep learning models, recent advancements in large language models (LLM) present new opportunities for enhancing prediction accuracy. This study systematically evaluates 18 LLM variants (including BERT, RoBERTa, DeBERTa-v3, ELECTRA, and XLNet for encoder models; GPT-3.5-turbo, GPT-4o-mini, Llama 3.1 8B, Llama 3.1 8B Instruct and DeepSeek-V3 for decoder models) against traditional methods (CNN/LSTM-BERT/GloVe). Our experiments reveal two key insights: (1) Incorporating creator profiles increases unfine-tuned decoder-only models' overall accuracy by 10.8 percent though it may introduce bias; (2) LoRA fine-tuned Llama 3.1 8B Instruct further improve performance, reaching 79 percent accuracy and significantly enhancing recall for approved reports (76.1 percent vs. LSTM-GLOVE's 64.1 percent), outperforming traditional methods by 5 percent under strict chronological evaluation and effectively addressing class imbalance issues. These findings establish LLM as a superior solution for ERAP, demonstrating their potential to streamline software maintenance workflows and improve decision-making in real-world development environments. We also investigated and summarized the ER cases where the large models underperformed, providing valuable directions for future research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the application of Large Language Models (LLMs) to the task of Enhancement Report Approval Prediction (ERAP).  It compares the performance of various encoder-only (e.g., BERT, RoBERTa, ELECTRA) and decoder-only LLMs (e.g., GPT, Llama, DeepSeek) against traditional machine learning methods (CNN, LSTM) on a dataset of Bugzilla enhancement reports. The study explores different prompting strategies, fine-tuning techniques (LoRA), and the impact of incorporating creator profiles. The key findings indicate that fine-tuned Llama models, especially those incorporating creator profiles, outperform traditional methods and address class imbalance issues. The paper also analyzes scenarios where LLMs underperform, offering insights for future research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in being the first comprehensive empirical evaluation of LLMs for the ERAP task. While previous research has explored traditional ML and deep learning approaches, this paper bridges the gap by systematically assessing the performance of a wide array of both encoder and decoder LLMs. Comparing them to traditional approaches is also a novel aspect. It also studies failure cases.
*   **Significance:** ERAP is a relevant and practically important problem in software engineering. Automating the ER triage process has the potential to save significant development resources and improve user satisfaction. Demonstrating the effectiveness of LLMs in this context is significant because it suggests a pathway to more efficient and intelligent software maintenance workflows.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The study examines a diverse range of LLM architectures and models, including encoder-only and decoder-only variants, and tests multiple configurations with fine-tuning.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The paper employs a strict chronological data splitting strategy to avoid data leakage and simulates realistic deployment scenarios. The hyperparameter tuning and data preprocessing are well-defined.
    *   **Practical Insights:**  The analysis of failure cases (misclassified ERs) provides valuable directions for future research by highlighting the limitations of LLMs in handling specific types of enhancement requests (e.g., those requiring in-depth domain knowledge or UI/UX considerations). The study of incorporating creator profiles is also a strong point. The statistical significance of the results is adequately established.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dataset Limitations:** While the Bugzilla dataset is commonly used, the data spans from 1997 to 2016. Since these models may have been trained on data containing at least some data from that time span, this poses a threat to validity.  A more recent dataset or a method to ensure data in the dataset has not been seen before (using a LLM to detect leakage) would strenghten the validity of the conclusions.
    *   **Limited Baselines:** While the choice of baselines is reasonable, a direct comparison to other LLM-based text classification methods, even if not specifically tailored to ERAP, would offer a broader perspective.
    *   **Generalizability:** The results are specific to the Bugzilla dataset and the types of software projects represented within it.  Generalizing the findings to other types of software or ER systems might require further validation.
    *   **Explanations for Failure Cases:** While the paper analyzes misclassified cases, the reasoning provided for *why* certain models fail is somewhat speculative. More concrete evidence (e.g., attention maps) could strengthen these explanations.
    *   **Computational Cost Consideration:** The paper doesn't deeply discuss the computational overhead associated with fine-tuning and deploying large language models, especially in contrast with the more lightweight traditional methods. A deeper cost-benefit analysis would be valuable from a practical adoption perspective.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper establishes a strong foundation for future research on LLMs for ERAP. The findings may encourage developers to explore LLM-based solutions for automating software maintenance tasks. The identified failure cases provide a roadmap for improving the robustness and reliability of LLM-based ERAP systems.

**Overall:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by systematically evaluating LLMs on ERAP. While some limitations exist, the rigorous methodology and practical insights provide a valuable starting point for future research and real-world applications.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**  The paper offers a novel and significant contribution through its comprehensive evaluation of LLMs on ERAP. The methodology is sound, and the analysis provides practical insights. However, the dataset's age and the speculative nature of some of the failure case explanations limit the overall impact. Addressing these shortcomings could elevate the paper to an even higher level of significance.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### CipherMind: The Longest Codebook in the World
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15117v1)
- **Authors**: Ming Nie, Zhixiong Yang, Bingsheng Wei
- **Abstract**: In recent years, the widespread application of large language models has inspired us to consider using inference for communication encryption. We therefore propose CipherMind, which utilizes intermediate results from deterministic fine-tuning of large model inferences as transmission content. The semantic parameters of large models exhibit characteristics like opaque underlying implementations and weak interpretability, thus enabling their use as an encryption method for data transmission. This communication paradigm can be applied in scenarios like intra-gateway transmission, and theoretically, it can be implemented using any large model as its foundation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "CipherMind," a novel cryptographic communication framework that leverages the intermediate results of large language model (LLM) inference as a form of encryption. The core idea is to use deterministic fine-tuning on LLMs and extract the model's intermediate layer outputs during inference as the "ciphertext." The paper proposes a "heterogeneous twin" fine-tuning method to ensure that the sender and receiver have identical post-tuning parameters. It also integrates a Cipher Block Chaining (CBC) encryption process for added security. The authors present preliminary experiments using Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct models to demonstrate the feasibility of their approach and analyze the information transfer capability and resistance to collision attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The idea of using intermediate outputs from LLMs as a form of encryption is somewhat novel. Existing cryptographic techniques rarely involve exploiting the internal workings of neural networks directly for encryption. The heterogeneous twin fine-tuning approach to ensure synchronized models between communicating parties is also an interesting implementation detail.

**Significance:** The paper's significance is currently limited. While the concept is intriguing, the experimental results and analysis are preliminary. The paper doesn't demonstrate a level of security comparable to established cryptographic methods. The reliance on proprietary models (Qwen2.5-0.5B) and the custom datasets for fine-tuning also reduces the immediate generalizability of the work. The lack of any rigorous security analysis, such as a discussion of key space or potential attack vectors beyond the collision experiments, is a major weakness.

**Strengths:**

*   **Innovative Concept:** The idea of utilizing LLMs in the encryption process represents an original direction for cryptographic research.
*   **Practical Implementation Details:** The paper addresses the practical challenges of model synchronization through the "heterogeneous twin" approach and incorporates CBC encryption to enhance security.
*   **Proof-of-Concept Experiments:** The experiments provide initial evidence that the approach is feasible and demonstrates information transfer capability.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Security Analysis:** The paper lacks a rigorous security analysis to demonstrate the cryptographic strength of CipherMind. Collision experiments are insufficient to validate the security. No mention is made of standard cryptographic metrics and testing.
*   **Small Model and Dataset Size:** The use of a small model (Qwen2.5-0.5B) and custom datasets limits the generalizability and scalability of the results. It is unclear if this approach scales to larger, more powerful models and datasets.
*   **Dependency on Deterministic Fine-tuning:** The reliance on deterministic fine-tuning creates a potential vulnerability. Any compromise in the fine-tuning process could compromise the entire system. The chosen fine-tuning algorithm and configuration is also likely to determine the final security strength.
*   **Lack of Comparison to Existing Methods:** The paper doesn't compare CipherMind's performance and security to existing encryption methods, making it difficult to assess its relative advantages and disadvantages.
*   **High Deployment Costs**: The use of LLMs requires considerable computational power which may hinder adoption.
*   **Limited Scope**: The paper focuses heavily on technical aspects with little to no discussion regarding ethical and societal aspects.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper could influence future research in applying machine learning techniques for cryptographic purposes. However, it's more likely to serve as a preliminary exploration of a novel concept rather than a direct basis for practical cryptographic systems without substantial improvements in security analysis and scalability.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's novelty is the primary reason for its score being above a 1. The core idea of using LLM internal states for encryption is creative. However, the lack of rigorous security analysis, limited scalability, and reliance on proprietary models significantly limit its practical significance and impact. While the preliminary experiments are encouraging, they do not establish that CipherMind offers any real-world security advantages over existing cryptographic methods. Therefore, a rating indicating a need for significant further development is appropriate.

**Score: 4**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 4/10

### CKD-EHR:Clinical Knowledge Distillation for Electronic Health Records
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15118v1)
- **Authors**: Junke Wang, Hongshun Ling, Li Zhang, Longqian Zhang, Fang Wang, Yuan Gao, Zhi Li
- **Abstract**: Electronic Health Records (EHR)-based disease prediction models have demonstrated significant clinical value in promoting precision medicine and enabling early intervention. However, existing large language models face two major challenges: insufficient representation of medical knowledge and low efficiency in clinical deployment. To address these challenges, this study proposes the CKD-EHR (Clinical Knowledge Distillation for EHR) framework, which achieves efficient and accurate disease risk prediction through knowledge distillation techniques. Specifically, the large language model Qwen2.5-7B is first fine-tuned on medical knowledge-enhanced data to serve as the teacher model.It then generates interpretable soft labels through a multi-granularity attention distillation mechanism. Finally, the distilled knowledge is transferred to a lightweight BERT student model. Experimental results show that on the MIMIC-III dataset, CKD-EHR significantly outperforms the baseline model:diagnostic accuracy is increased by 9%, F1-score is improved by 27%, and a 22.2 times inference speedup is achieved. This innovative solution not only greatly improves resource utilization efficiency but also significantly enhances the accuracy and timeliness of diagnosis, providing a practical technical approach for resource optimization in clinical settings. The code and data for this research are available athttps://github.com/209506702/CKD_EHR.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a Clinical Knowledge Distillation for Electronic Health Records (CKD-EHR) framework to improve disease prediction models. The framework addresses the challenges of insufficient medical knowledge representation and low deployment efficiency in large language models (LLMs). CKD-EHR integrates data augmentation with knowledge distillation. It fine-tunes a large language model (Qwen2.5-7B) on medical knowledge-enhanced data as a teacher model, generates interpretable soft labels using a multi-granularity attention distillation mechanism, and transfers the distilled knowledge to a lightweight BERT student model. A Multi-Label Adaptive Projection Head (MLAPH) is used to produce soft labels for multi-label disease prediction. Experiments on the MIMIC-III dataset show improvements in diagnostic accuracy, F1-score, and inference speedup compared to baseline models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a Practical Problem:**  The paper tackles the real-world challenges of deploying LLMs in healthcare, specifically addressing resource limitations and the need for specialized medical knowledge.
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** CKD-EHR combines several techniques effectively: data augmentation with EADF, knowledge distillation, efficient fine-tuning with LoRA, and a multi-label adaptive projection head.  The combination of these elements appears well-integrated.
    *   **Demonstrated Improvements:** The experimental results show substantial improvements over existing baselines on the MIMIC-III dataset, including both accuracy and efficiency metrics.
    *   **Well-defined Contributions:** The paper clearly states its contributions, including the integration of data augmentation with knowledge distillation and the distillation of an LLM into a BERT model.
    *   **Good Ablation Studies:** The inclusion of ablation studies to test the impact of the different components of the framework and probability calculation methods demonstrates the effectiveness of the chosen approach.
    *   **Detailed Prompt Design:** Appending the data prompts and their design showcase the effectiveness of the model.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The evaluation is primarily based on the MIMIC-III dataset, which while widely used, may not represent the diversity of all EHR data.  The paper acknowledges this limitation and the need to extend it to multi-modal datasets. Further testing on other datasets would strengthen the conclusions.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is effective, the individual components (knowledge distillation, LoRA, data augmentation) are not entirely novel. The novelty lies more in the specific application and integration within the EHR domain.
    *   **Dependency on Qwen2.5-7B Performance:** The effectiveness of the student model is inherently tied to the performance of the teacher model (Qwen2.5-7B). If the teacher model has limitations, the distilled knowledge will also be limited.
    *   **Justification of MLAPH:** Although results are presented, a deeper explanation of why MLAPH is crucial for performance compared to standard techniques or justifications of why this would not work well in standard scenarios would boost the significance.
    *  **Clarity:** More intuitive visual aids/diagrams would help to convey and highlight the novelty and significance.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Practical Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly improve the practicality of using LLMs in healthcare by enabling more efficient and accurate disease prediction. The resource efficiency is especially crucial for real-world deployment.
    *   **Contribution to Knowledge Distillation in Healthcare:** It provides a valuable framework for applying knowledge distillation techniques to EHR data, potentially inspiring further research in this area.
    *  **Guidance for Future Research:** Demonstrating the effectiveness and utility of soft data labels.

**Score and Justification:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, I would assign a score of **7**.

*   The paper tackles a practically relevant problem and demonstrates significant improvements compared to existing methods.
*   The limitations in generalizability and incremental novelty prevent a higher score. The individual methods have been proposed/implemented before and are combined and implemented. While the approach provides good benefits, the significance is somewhat limited by this fact.
*   The framework makes a valuable contribution to the field of applying LLMs to healthcare. Demonstrating how the models perform against different approaches to model compression would strengthen significance.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generative thermodynamic computing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15121v1)
- **Authors**: Stephen Whitelam
- **Abstract**: We introduce a generative modeling framework for thermodynamic computing, in which structured data is synthesized from noise by the natural time evolution of a physical system governed by Langevin dynamics. While conventional diffusion models use neural networks to perform denoising, here the information needed to generate structure from noise is encoded by the dynamics of a thermodynamic system. Training proceeds by maximizing the probability with which the computer generates the reverse of a noising trajectory, which ensures that the computer generates data with minimal heat emission. We demonstrate this framework within a digital simulation of a thermodynamic computer. If realized in analog hardware, such a system would function as a generative model that produces structured samples without the need for artificially-injected noise or active control of denoising.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, with a focus on rigor and justification for the score.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a generative modeling framework using thermodynamic computing, drawing parallels to diffusion models. Instead of relying on neural networks and artificially generated noise for denoising (as in conventional diffusion models), it proposes encoding the information needed to generate structure within the dynamics of a thermodynamic system governed by Langevin equations. The training process involves maximizing the probability that the "computer" (a simulated thermodynamic system) generates the reverse of a noising trajectory, minimizing heat emission. The authors demonstrate this within a digital simulation using a model thermodynamic computer and show that it can generate MNIST-like digits from noisy inputs.  The paper emphasizes the potential of realizing such a system in analog hardware, where the denoising process would be physically enacted rather than simulated. The training process is also linked to minimizing thermodynamic irreversibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The core idea of using a *thermodynamic system's natural dynamics for generative modeling* is interesting and potentially novel.  The crucial distinction is replacing the explicit denoising network of diffusion models with the inherent dynamics of a physical system governed by the Langevin equation. The explicit connection made to Boltzmann machines, which are related to the model, makes the novelty a relative one. This is a *Langevin Computer*, instead of the standard form.

**Significance:**

*   **Potential for Energy Efficiency:**  The claim of potentially higher energy efficiency (by exploiting natural physical processes) is a significant motivator.  If realized in analog hardware, it could offer advantages over energy-intensive digital implementations of generative models.  However, *this is purely speculative at this point*.  There's no concrete analysis of energy efficiency.
*   **Physical Grounding of Generative Models:**  The paper provides a step towards physically grounded machine learning.  Connecting generative models to fundamental thermodynamic principles has the potential to deepen our understanding of these models and lead to new architectures.
*   **Link to Irreversibility:**  The demonstration that training minimizes thermodynamic irreversibility is a compelling connection between machine learning and physics. This provides a physical interpretation to the optimization process in generative modeling.
*   **Theoretical Nature:** The paper remains largely theoretical and simulation-based. The real-world challenges of building and controlling such an analog thermodynamic computer are substantial.  The demonstration on a toy problem (MNIST digits) is a proof-of-concept, but doesn't demonstrate significant advantage.

**Strengths:**

*   Clearly articulated concept.
*   Direct analogies to diffusion models.
*   Connection to fundamental physics (thermodynamics).
*   Demonstration with digital simulation.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Lack of experimental validation or even a detailed proposal for physical implementation. This is key because the advantage lies in the physical realization rather than the simulation.
*   No comparative analysis of energy efficiency versus standard diffusion models.
*   The use of MNIST as a demonstration example is a bit cliché. Stronger datasets would be better.
*   The description of specific hardware to realize such a computer is brief and doesn't provide concrete design details.
*   The performance of the computer in its generation ability is not deeply analyzed nor explained.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper could inspire researchers to explore alternative, physically grounded approaches to machine learning. If the concept proves feasible in hardware, it could lead to a new class of energy-efficient generative models. However, the impact hinges on overcoming significant experimental and engineering challenges.

**Score:**

Score: 6

**Rationale for the score:**

A score of 6 reflects the paper's interesting concept and its potential significance, balanced against its current theoretical and simulation-based status. The novelty, while present, is incremental and not radical. The paper presents a compelling vision, but *the lack of experimental validation significantly limits its current impact*. While connecting the optimization process to minimization of irreversibility is a strength, it's not sufficient to push the score higher without supporting evidence. The claim of energy efficiency is just a claim, without rigorous analysis. A successful hardware implementation and a comparative energy analysis would significantly elevate the impact of this work.

- **Classification**: cond-mat.stat-mech
- **Score**: 6/10

### eLLM: Elastic Memory Management Framework for Efficient LLM Serving
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15155v1)
- **Authors**: Jiale Xu, Rui Zhang, Yi Xiong, Cong Guo, Zihan Liu, Yangjie Zhou, Weiming Hu, Hao Wu, Changxu Shao, Ziqing Wang, Yongjie Yuan, Junping Zhao, Minyi Guo, Jingwen Leng
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models are increasingly being deployed in datacenters. Serving these models requires careful memory management, as their memory usage includes static weights, dynamic activations, and key-value caches. While static weights are constant and predictable, dynamic components such as activations and KV caches change frequently during runtime, presenting significant challenges for efficient memory management. Modern LLM serving systems typically handle runtime memory and KV caches at distinct abstraction levels: runtime memory management relies on static tensor abstractions, whereas KV caches utilize a page table-based virtualization layer built on top of the tensor abstraction. This virtualization dynamically manages KV caches to mitigate memory fragmentation. However, this dual-level approach fundamentally isolates runtime memory and KV cache management, resulting in suboptimal memory utilization under dynamic workloads, which can lead to a nearly 20% drop in throughput. To address these limitations, we propose eLLM, an elastic memory management framework inspired by the classical memory ballooning mechanism in operating systems. The core components of eLLM include: (1) Virtual Tensor Abstraction, which decouples the virtual address space of tensors from the physical GPU memory, creating a unified and flexible memory pool; (2) an Elastic Memory Mechanism that dynamically adjusts memory allocation through runtime memory inflation and deflation, leveraging CPU memory as an extensible buffer; and (3) a Lightweight Scheduling Strategy employing SLO-aware policies to optimize memory utilization and effectively balance performance trade-offs under stringent SLO constraints. Comprehensive evaluations demonstrate that eLLM significantly outperforms state-of-the-art systems, 2.32x higher decoding throughput, and supporting 3x larger batch sizes for 128K-token inputs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "eLLM: Elastic Memory Management Framework for Efficient LLM Serving":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces eLLM, an elastic memory management framework designed to improve the efficiency of serving large language models (LLMs).  eLLM addresses the problem of memory isolation between runtime memory (activations) and KV caches in existing LLM serving systems (like vLLM).  It achieves this through three main components: (1) a virtual tensor abstraction that decouples virtual address spaces from physical GPU memory, creating a unified memory pool; (2) an elastic memory mechanism that dynamically adjusts memory allocation using inflation/deflation and leveraging CPU memory as an extension; and (3) a lightweight scheduling strategy that optimizes memory usage and balances performance trade-offs under Service Level Objective (SLO) constraints. The authors demonstrate that eLLM outperforms existing systems, achieving higher throughput and supporting larger batch sizes for long-context inputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core idea of unifying memory management for activations and KV caches under a single elastic framework is a significant step forward, especially considering the evolving demands of longer context lengths and newer model architectures (like those using GQA, MLA and Mamba).  While vLLM addressed KV cache fragmentation effectively, eLLM's holistic approach to memory management appears to be a novel and timely contribution.  The inspiration from OS-level memory ballooning is clever and well-motivated. The design of the virtual tensor abstraction and the elastic memory mechanisms, although building upon existing concepts, are tailored specifically to the nuances of LLM serving.  The lightweight scheduling strategy complements the memory management and provides an efficient way to handle SLO requirements.

**Significance:**

The demonstrated performance improvements (2.32x higher decoding throughput, 3x larger batch sizes) are substantial and practically important. The ability to serve LLMs more efficiently translates to cost savings, reduced latency for end-users, and the potential to deploy larger, more powerful models. The ablation study provides insights into the effectiveness of different components of eLLM. The work addresses a genuine and pressing challenge in the LLM serving space: memory utilization and performance degradation due to memory isolation and fragmentation.

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Definition:** Clearly identifies the limitations of current LLM serving systems, particularly the issue of memory isolation.
*   **Design:**  Well-structured and clearly explained system design with three distinct and interconnected components.
*   **Evaluation:** Comprehensive evaluation across various models, workloads, and metrics. The comparisons against strong baselines (vLLM, DistServe) are convincing. Ablation studies demonstrate the contribution of individual components.
*   **Practical Relevance:**  The proposed framework tackles a real-world problem and offers tangible benefits in terms of performance and efficiency.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Overhead of CPU Offloading:** While CPU offloading is presented as a benefit, a deeper analysis of its overhead and its interaction with different network bandwidths would strengthen the paper.  The impact on end-to-end latency for certain workloads should be discussed.
*   **Generality:** While the evaluation includes several models, more evidence regarding the framework's adaptability to diverse model architectures (e.g., models with vastly different activation profiles) would make the work more impactful. The degree to which the system requires model-specific tuning should be explicitly stated.
*   **VMM API Overhead:** In the online serving evaluation, the 1% to 5% overhead stemming from VMM operation time merits a bit more discussion and analysis, especially in the context of high-throughput serving. How this scales is crucial.

**Potential Influence:**

eLLM has the potential to significantly influence the design of future LLM serving systems. The idea of unifying memory management with elastic resource allocation could become a standard practice. The virtual tensor abstraction provides a flexible foundation for future optimizations and extensions. The framework's SLO-aware scheduling could be adapted to other resource management problems in LLM serving.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and practically significant solution to a key challenge in LLM serving. The framework is well-designed, thoroughly evaluated, and demonstrates substantial performance improvements. While some aspects, like the overhead of CPU offloading and generality could be explored further, the overall contribution is strong.  It is a solid, well-executed piece of research that is likely to have a lasting impact on the field.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 8/10

### Robust Instant Policy: Leveraging Student's t-Regression Model for Robust In-context Imitation Learning of Robot Manipulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15157v1)
- **Authors**: Hanbit Oh, Andrea M. Salcedo-Vázquez, Ixchel G. Ramirez-Alpizar, Yukiyasu Domae
- **Abstract**: Imitation learning (IL) aims to enable robots to perform tasks autonomously by observing a few human demonstrations. Recently, a variant of IL, called In-Context IL, utilized off-the-shelf large language models (LLMs) as instant policies that understand the context from a few given demonstrations to perform a new task, rather than explicitly updating network models with large-scale demonstrations. However, its reliability in the robotics domain is undermined by hallucination issues such as LLM-based instant policy, which occasionally generates poor trajectories that deviate from the given demonstrations. To alleviate this problem, we propose a new robust in-context imitation learning algorithm called the robust instant policy (RIP), which utilizes a Student's t-regression model to be robust against the hallucinated trajectories of instant policies to allow reliable trajectory generation. Specifically, RIP generates several candidate robot trajectories to complete a given task from an LLM and aggregates them using the Student's t-distribution, which is beneficial for ignoring outliers (i.e., hallucinations); thereby, a robust trajectory against hallucinations is generated. Our experiments, conducted in both simulated and real-world environments, show that RIP significantly outperforms state-of-the-art IL methods, with at least $26\%$ improvement in task success rates, particularly in low-data scenarios for everyday tasks. Video results available at https://sites.google.com/view/robustinstantpolicy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Robust Instant Policy: Leveraging Student's t-Regression Model for Robust In-context Imitation Learning of Robot Manipulation" addresses the problem of hallucinations in Large Language Model (LLM)-based in-context imitation learning (ICIL) for robot manipulation. The authors propose a novel approach called Robust Instant Policy (RIP), which uses a Student's t-regression model to aggregate multiple candidate robot trajectories generated by an LLM. The Student's t-distribution is used to robustly ignore outlier trajectories (hallucinations) generated by the LLM, leading to a more reliable final trajectory for the robot to execute. The paper presents experiments in both simulated and real-world environments, demonstrating that RIP outperforms state-of-the-art imitation learning methods, particularly in low-data scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the application of a Student's t-regression model to mitigate hallucinations in LLM-based ICIL for robot manipulation. While Student's t-distributions are a well-established statistical tool for outlier rejection, their specific use within the context of in-context learning and robotic trajectory generation seems relatively new.  The method builds upon the existing work of Keypoint Action Tokens (KAT) by Di Palo and Johns [7], using KAT as the LLM-based instant policy, then introducing the outlier rejection mechanism.  The improvement over KAT is clearly demonstrated empirically.

*   **Significance:** Hallucinations are a well-known issue in LLMs, and their presence undermines the reliability of LLM-based systems in safety-critical applications like robotics. By providing a mechanism to improve the robustness of LLM-based robot control, the paper addresses a practically important problem.  The performance gains (up to 26% improvement in task success rates) are significant, especially given the low-data settings considered.  The method also is relatively easy to implement atop existing LLM-based instant policies.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is clearly written and well-organized.
    *   The problem is well-motivated.
    *   The proposed solution is technically sound and relatively straightforward to implement.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough, covering both simulation and real-world settings.
    *   The comparison with state-of-the-art methods is comprehensive.
    *   The ablation studies analyze the impact of key design parameters (number of queries, degrees of freedom) of the Student's t-distribution.
    *   The downsampling technique is effective, particularly for tasks involving gripper actions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on the pretrained vision transformer (DINO) and the initial KAT policy limits the method's generalizability to environments where these components perform well.
    *   The computational complexity of the LLM inference, even with parallelization, could be a limitation in real-time applications that require faster response times, though this is less a weakness of *this paper specifically* and more a general weakness of LLM-based methods.
    *   While the ablation studies explore the impact of Q and ν, a more in-depth theoretical justification for selecting these values would be beneficial. Currently, the selection is primarily driven by empirical results.
    *   The paper mostly focuses on relatively simple manipulation tasks. It is unclear how well RIP would scale to more complex, long-horizon tasks.
    *   There is no explicit mechanism in RIP to recognize and understand *why* a particular trajectory is a hallucination. This means the policy learns to ignore outliers but does not learn from them in a way that improves future LLM response quality.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in several areas:
    *   ICIL for robotics
    *   Robust control using LLMs
    *   Outlier rejection techniques in robot learning

*   **Score Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and effective approach to improving the robustness of LLM-based ICIL. While the method has some limitations, it addresses a relevant and important problem in the field. The clear presentation, thorough evaluation, and significant performance gains suggest that the paper will have a positive impact on the community.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Echo-DND: A dual noise diffusion model for robust and precise left ventricle segmentation in echocardiography
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15166v1)
- **Authors**: Abdur Rahman, Keerthiveena Balraj, Manojkumar Ramteke, Anurag Singh Rathore
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in diffusion probabilistic models (DPMs) have revolutionized image processing, demonstrating significant potential in medical applications. Accurate segmentation of the left ventricle (LV) in echocardiograms is crucial for diagnostic procedures and necessary treatments. However, ultrasound images are notoriously noisy with low contrast and ambiguous LV boundaries, thereby complicating the segmentation process. To address these challenges, this paper introduces Echo-DND, a novel dual-noise diffusion model specifically designed for this task. Echo-DND leverages a unique combination of Gaussian and Bernoulli noises. It also incorporates a multi-scale fusion conditioning module to improve segmentation precision. Furthermore, it utilizes spatial coherence calibration to maintain spatial integrity in segmentation masks. The model's performance was rigorously validated on the CAMUS and EchoNet-Dynamic datasets. Extensive evaluations demonstrate that the proposed framework outperforms existing SOTA models. It achieves high Dice scores of 0.962 and 0.939 on these datasets, respectively. The proposed Echo-DND model establishes a new standard in echocardiogram segmentation, and its architecture holds promise for broader applicability in other medical imaging tasks, potentially improving diagnostic accuracy across various medical domains. Project page: https://abdur75648.github.io/Echo-DND
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Echo-DND," a novel dual-noise diffusion model for accurate segmentation of the left ventricle (LV) in echocardiography images.  The model combines Gaussian and Bernoulli noise in the diffusion process, utilizes a multi-scale fusion conditioning module (MFCM) to capture both global and local details, and incorporates a spatial coherence calibration (SCC) technique to maintain spatial integrity of segmentation masks.  The authors demonstrate that Echo-DND achieves state-of-the-art performance on the CAMUS and EchoNet-Dynamic datasets, surpassing existing methods in terms of Dice score. Ablation studies are performed to validate the importance of the various components. The paper concludes that Echo-DND offers a robust solution for LV segmentation in echocardiography and has potential for broader application in medical imaging.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper has several novel elements:

*   **Dual-Noise Diffusion:**  The key innovation is the combination of Gaussian and Bernoulli noise within a single diffusion framework. This is presented as particularly suited to binary segmentation tasks, taking into account both gaussian variations and binary variations in the LV.
*   **Multi-Scale Fusion Conditioning Module (MFCM):** While multi-scale feature fusion isn't entirely new, the application of HRNet principles within the diffusion framework is a distinct architectural choice.
*   **Spatial Coherence Calibration (SCC):** The integration of CNN-based spatial coherence loss during the diffusion process aims to combine the advantages of both CNNs and DPMs.

**Significance:**

*   **Improved Performance:** The paper demonstrates significant performance improvements on two standard echocardiography datasets. This translates directly to potentially better clinical workflows by decreasing errors, improving efficiency, and enabling better diagnoses in cardiology.
*   **Robustness in Challenging Conditions:** The paper emphasizes that Echo-DND excels in situations with low contrast and ambiguous LV boundaries. These scenarios can limit the effectiveness of other segmentation techniques.
*   **Potential for Generalization:** While primarily focused on echocardiography, the underlying architecture principles (dual-noise diffusion, MFCM, SCC) could be transferred to other medical image segmentation problems.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Statement and Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of LV segmentation in echocardiography and motivates the need for a more robust and accurate technique.
*   **Comprehensive Methodology:**  The description of the Echo-DND architecture, training process, and experimental setup is detailed and well-structured.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The performance comparisons against multiple state-of-the-art methods on standard datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach. Ablation studies further strengthen the claims.
*   **Detailed Analysis:**  The paper provides detailed analysis including specific cases with challenges that are encountered in clinical settings and how Echo-DND addresses these challenges.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** The paper acknowledges that diffusion models in general have a high computational cost. While there are efforts mentioned, the paper does not offer any concrete steps towards reducing computational requirements making it less accessible.
*  **Limited Discussion of Failure Cases:** Figure 5 identifies a number of instances where the algorithm fails, but it does not provide a substantial analysis on *why* the algorithm fails on these datasets. More detail around this point would improve the paper.
*   **Runtime Analysis:** While the inference time is mentioned, it does not provide a comparison of runtime relative to other standard methods in the field.
*   **Generalizability:** The performance analysis is limited to echocardiography datasets. There are no demonstrations on other medical imaging modalities that would indicate that the algorithm is broadly useful.

**Justification for the Score:**

Based on the above, I assign a score of **7**.

**Rationale:**

Echo-DND presents a set of non-trivial technical advancements, leading to quantitatively and qualitatively significant improvements over other approaches in a challenging medical imaging segmentation task. The dual-noise approach is potentially useful for more discrete medical imaging applications.

However, the relatively high computational cost of diffusion models, the lack of a comparison to previous results and the lack of evidence of any generalizability to broader sets of applications limits the paper's impact in the short term. The future work directions mentioned by the authors could further increase the impact of the work if they manage to successfully accomplish this.

Therefore, while demonstrating tangible improvements and introducing innovative elements, the limitations described above keep the paper from being considered a truly exceptional contribution (scores of 8, 9, or 10).

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 0/10

### From LLMs to MLLMs to Agents: A Survey of Emerging Paradigms in Jailbreak Attacks and Defenses within LLM Ecosystem
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15170v1)
- **Authors**: Yanxu Mao, Tiehan Cui, Peipei Liu, Datao You, Hongsong Zhu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are rapidly evolving from single-modal systems to multimodal LLMs and intelligent agents, significantly expanding their capabilities while introducing increasingly severe security risks. This paper presents a systematic survey of the growing complexity of jailbreak attacks and corresponding defense mechanisms within the expanding LLM ecosystem. We first trace the developmental trajectory from LLMs to MLLMs and Agents, highlighting the core security challenges emerging at each stage. Next, we categorize mainstream jailbreak techniques from both the attack impact and visibility perspectives, and provide a comprehensive analysis of representative attack methods, related datasets, and evaluation metrics. On the defense side, we organize existing strategies based on response timing and technical approach, offering a structured understanding of their applicability and implementation. Furthermore, we identify key limitations in existing surveys, such as insufficient attention to agent-specific security issues, the absence of a clear taxonomy for hybrid jailbreak methods, a lack of detailed analysis of experimental setups, and outdated coverage of recent advancements. To address these limitations, we provide an updated synthesis of recent work and outline future research directions in areas such as dataset construction, evaluation framework optimization, and strategy generalization. Our study seeks to enhance the understanding of jailbreak mechanisms and facilitate the advancement of more resilient and adaptive defense strategies in the context of ever more capable LLMs.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

The paper provides a comprehensive survey of jailbreak attacks and defense mechanisms in the evolving LLM ecosystem, encompassing single-modal LLMs, multimodal LLMs (MLLMs), and AI agents. It traces the development of these systems, categorizes jailbreak techniques based on attack impact and visibility, analyzes representative attack methods, datasets, and evaluation metrics, and organizes defense strategies by response timing and technical approach. It also identifies key limitations in existing surveys and proposes future research directions, including dataset construction, evaluation framework optimization, and strategy generalization. The survey aims to enhance the understanding of jailbreak mechanisms and facilitate the development of resilient defense strategies in increasingly capable LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty and Significance:** While the paper is a thorough survey and attempts a comprehensive treatment of the jailbreak landscape, its novelty is somewhat limited. Several other surveys cover similar ground. The key differentiating factor lies in the inclusion of AI agents as a critical component of the LLM ecosystem. However, the analysis of agent-specific security issues, as claimed, could be significantly deeper. The paper does a decent job of categorizing attacks and defenses and identifying limitations of existing surveys, but the proposed future directions are relatively standard suggestions for the field.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The paper covers a broad range of topics, from the evolution of LLMs to specific attack techniques and defense strategies, providing a valuable overview of the field.
*   **Clear Organization:** The paper is well-structured, making it easy to follow the arguments and analyses. The categorization of jailbreak techniques and defense strategies is particularly helpful.
*   **Identification of Limitations:** The paper identifies several limitations in existing surveys, such as the lack of focus on agents and inadequate taxonomy of hybrid methods, which is important for guiding future research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Depth of Agent Analysis:** While the paper highlights agents as a key area, the agent-specific analysis isn't as deep as one might expect. The paper mentions the expanded attack surface, but a more in-depth examination of the vulnerabilities specific to agent architectures, their planning components, and tool integrations would have been highly valuable.
*   **Hybrid Jailbreak Taxonomy:** The paper claims a lack of clear taxonomy for hybrid jailbreak methods, but it doesn't present a substantially novel or significantly improved taxonomy in its analysis.
*   **Experimental Setup Analysis:** Although the abstract claimed lack of detailed analysis of experimental setups as a limitation to be addressed, this is barely addressed.
*   **Future Directions:** The future research directions are fairly generic.  More specific recommendations tied directly to the survey's findings, especially those relevant to the integration of MLLMs and Agents, would enhance the paper's impact.
*   **Lack of Quantitative Analysis:** The survey is largely qualitative, lacking extensive quantitative comparisons or meta-analyses of the effectiveness of different attack and defense techniques based on available data. Including even a small-scale meta-analysis or statistical comparison of the available experimental results would have enhanced the practical value of the survey.
* **Redundancy** A number of the figures in this document have been described as the same type of object. This makes the survey feel artificially large without containing significantly more information.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance is primarily in providing a consolidated overview of the evolving threat landscape in LLMs and the initial efforts to address the security challenges in more complex systems such as MLLMs and Agents. It offers a useful starting point for researchers entering the field, but its limited novelty restricts its long-term influence. The inclusion of agents elevates its significance slightly compared to solely focusing on LLMs, but the depth of the agent-specific analysis remains a point for improvement. It's a good resource for understanding the state of the art as of its publication date, but not a landmark paper that shifts the field's trajectory.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Accessible Gesture-Driven Augmented Reality Interaction System
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15189v1)
- **Authors**: Yikan Wang
- **Abstract**: Augmented reality (AR) offers immersive interaction but remains inaccessible for users with motor impairments or limited dexterity due to reliance on precise input methods. This study proposes a gesture-based interaction system for AR environments, leveraging deep learning to recognize hand and body gestures from wearable sensors and cameras, adapting interfaces to user capabilities. The system employs vision transformers (ViTs), temporal convolutional networks (TCNs), and graph attention networks (GATs) for gesture processing, with federated learning ensuring privacy-preserving model training across diverse users. Reinforcement learning optimizes interface elements like menu layouts and interaction modes. Experiments demonstrate a 20% improvement in task completion efficiency and a 25% increase in user satisfaction for motor-impaired users compared to baseline AR systems. This approach enhances AR accessibility and scalability. Keywords: Deep learning, Federated learning, Gesture recognition, Augmented reality, Accessibility, Human-computer interaction
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary of the paper and a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, followed by a justified score.

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a gesture-based augmented reality (AR) interaction system designed to be accessible for users with motor impairments. It leverages deep learning techniques, including vision transformers (ViTs), temporal convolutional networks (TCNs), and graph attention networks (GATs), to recognize hand and body gestures from multimodal inputs (camera, accelerometer, EMG). Federated learning is employed to train the models in a privacy-preserving manner across diverse users, and reinforcement learning is used to dynamically optimize AR interface elements based on user capabilities and preferences. The system is evaluated using the UCI Gesture Recognition Dataset, and the results demonstrate improvements in gesture recognition accuracy, task completion efficiency, and user satisfaction compared to baseline AR systems and static interfaces.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses an important problem:** The paper tackles the significant issue of AR inaccessibility for individuals with motor impairments, a frequently overlooked aspect in AR development.
    *   **Multimodal approach:** The use of multimodal inputs (visual, accelerometer, EMG) for gesture recognition is a strong feature, as it can potentially improve robustness and accuracy, particularly in challenging conditions or for users with varying motor capabilities.
    *   **Advanced deep learning techniques:** The application of ViTs, TCNs, and GATs demonstrates an awareness of recent advances in deep learning and an attempt to leverage them for gesture recognition.
    *   **Privacy-preserving federated learning:** The incorporation of federated learning is a valuable addition, addressing privacy concerns that are crucial in user-specific data training.
    *   **Adaptive interface optimization:**  The use of reinforcement learning to dynamically adjust the AR interface based on user abilities is a key contribution, allowing for personalized and more effective interaction.
    *   **Experimental validation:** The paper provides experimental results based on a publicly available dataset, which lends credibility to the findings.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental novelty:** While the combination of techniques is interesting, each individual component (gesture recognition, federated learning, RL-based adaptation) has been explored previously in the context of HCI or AR. The novelty lies primarily in the specific integration of these established methods within the context of AR accessibility. The paper needs a stronger case to be made about the novelty beyond integration.
    *   **Dataset limitations:**  The UCI Gesture Recognition Dataset, while useful, may not fully capture the complexities of real-world AR usage scenarios or the diverse range of motor impairments. Further validation on a more realistic and diverse dataset would significantly strengthen the findings.
    *   **Limited detail on federated learning and RL implementation:**  The paper lacks sufficient detail on the implementation of federated learning (e.g., specific aggregation algorithms, communication protocols) and reinforcement learning (e.g., reward function tuning, exploration-exploitation strategies). A more detailed description would allow for better reproducibility and evaluation of the system.
    *   **User study:**  The reliance on the UCI dataset is also limiting in that it does not represent a real user study, especially considering that the target audience has motor impairments. Involving end users would significantly enhance the impact of the work.
    *   **Depth of analysis:** While the results show improvements, a more in-depth analysis of the specific gestures that are recognized well/poorly and the types of interface adaptations that are most effective would provide valuable insights.
    *   **Lack of comparison to state of the art accessibility interfaces for AR.**: While comparison to baselines is useful, the results should be validated against state-of-the-art accessible interaction methods for AR.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper has the potential to contribute to the development of more inclusive and accessible AR systems, which could benefit a significant portion of the population.
    *   The combination of deep learning, federated learning, and reinforcement learning offers a promising approach to address the challenges of gesture recognition and interface adaptation in AR.
    *   The findings could inspire further research in the area of accessible AR interaction and inform the design of more user-friendly AR applications for individuals with motor impairments.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper addresses a relevant and important problem, utilizing a combination of established but powerful techniques to create a novel AR interaction system. While it does demonstrate performance improvements based on a public dataset, its novelty is primarily in the specific integration of existing methods. Furthermore, the paper could be significantly enhanced by more extensive experimentation with real users, details of federated learning and reinforcement learning implementations and comparisons against state of the art accessibility interface methodologies.

### HeurAgenix: Leveraging LLMs for Solving Complex Combinatorial Optimization Challenges
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15196v1)
- **Authors**: Xianliang Yang, Ling Zhang, Haolong Qian, Lei Song, Jiang Bian
- **Abstract**: Heuristic algorithms play a vital role in solving combinatorial optimization (CO) problems, yet traditional designs depend heavily on manual expertise and struggle to generalize across diverse instances. We introduce \textbf{HeurAgenix}, a two-stage hyper-heuristic framework powered by large language models (LLMs) that first evolves heuristics and then selects among them automatically. In the heuristic evolution phase, HeurAgenix leverages an LLM to compare seed heuristic solutions with higher-quality solutions and extract reusable evolution strategies. During problem solving, it dynamically picks the most promising heuristic for each problem state, guided by the LLM's perception ability. For flexibility, this selector can be either a state-of-the-art LLM or a fine-tuned lightweight model with lower inference cost. To mitigate the scarcity of reliable supervision caused by CO complexity, we fine-tune the lightweight heuristic selector with a dual-reward mechanism that jointly exploits singals from selection preferences and state perception, enabling robust selection under noisy annotations. Extensive experiments on canonical benchmarks show that HeurAgenix not only outperforms existing LLM-based hyper-heuristics but also matches or exceeds specialized solvers. Code is available at https://github.com/microsoft/HeurAgenix.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "HeurAgenix: Leveraging LLMs for Solving Complex Combinatorial Optimization Challenges":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HeurAgenix, a two-stage hyper-heuristic framework powered by large language models (LLMs) designed to solve complex combinatorial optimization (CO) problems.  The framework operates in two phases: (1) **Heuristic Evolution:** An LLM analyzes contrastive solution tuples generated from a seed heuristic to extract and refine evolution strategies.  (2) **Problem Solving:** An LLM, or a fine-tuned lightweight model, dynamically selects the most promising heuristic for each problem state, guided by its perception ability.  To address the scarcity of reliable supervision, the lightweight model is fine-tuned using a dual-reward mechanism combining selection preferences and state perception.  The authors demonstrate through extensive experiments on canonical benchmarks that HeurAgenix outperforms existing LLM-based hyper-heuristics and matches or surpasses specialized solvers. A key aspect is the elimination of the need for an external task-specific solver commonly found in other LLM-based approaches, enabling better generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits strong novelty along several dimensions.
    *   **End-to-End LLM Hyper-Heuristic:**  Unlike previous LLM-based approaches, HeurAgenix presents a truly end-to-end optimization paradigm.  It automates *both* the design/evolution of heuristics and their adaptive selection during problem-solving, without relying on a pre-existing, domain-specific solver or complex manually defined combination mechanisms.
    *   **Contrastive Data-Driven Evolution:**  The approach to heuristic evolution is innovative.  Rather than relying on manually crafted evolution strategies, HeurAgenix learns these strategies directly from contrastive solution tuples, effectively extracting knowledge from solution trajectories.
    *   **Adaptive Selection with TTS and Dual-Reward:** The adaptive selection mechanism leverages both an LLM for initial filtering *and* a test-time scaling (TTS) approach for refined candidate evaluation. The dual reward mechanism enhances robust training on potentially noisy signals, further bolstering performance, and mitigating the noisy reward environment of combinatorial optimization problems.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this work is significant.
    *   **Generalization and Scalability:** By eliminating the need for task-specific solvers and learning generic evolution strategies, HeurAgenix offers the potential to scale across diverse CO problems. This could significantly reduce the manual effort required to develop high-performing optimization algorithms for new problem domains.
    *   **Performance:** The empirical results convincingly demonstrate that HeurAgenix achieves state-of-the-art performance, matching or outperforming existing specialized solvers while requiring significantly less domain knowledge.
    *   **Practical Applicability:**  The framework's modular design, allows practitioners to choose between a more computationally expensive but powerful full LLM or a fine-tuned lightweight model to manage inference cost in real-world scenarios, significantly enhancing its practical application.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes a thorough empirical evaluation across a variety of CO benchmarks, providing strong evidence for the effectiveness of HeurAgenix.
    *   **Clear and Well-Structured Presentation:** The methodology is well-explained, and the paper is clearly written. The figures and tables are effective in illustrating the key concepts and results.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies, particularly those related to the dual-reward mechanism, provide valuable insights into the contribution of different components of the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the paper addresses inference cost with lightweight models, the heuristic evolution phase likely demands substantial computational resources due to its dependence on LLM reasoning. Quantifying and optimizing this evolutionary cost would be valuable. The number of API calls is high (2000), and potentially prohibitive.
    *   **Limited LLM Backbone Exploration:** The paper relies primarily on GPT-4o and Qwen-7B. Exploring HeurAgenix with other LLMs, both open-source and closed-source, would further demonstrate its robustness and adaptability. The limited exploration of LLM backbones could limit the broader applicability of the claims made.

*   **Potential Influence:** HeurAgenix is positioned to be a highly influential work in the intersection of LLMs and combinatorial optimization. It establishes a new paradigm for automated heuristic design and adaptive selection, opening doors for future research on:
    *   Efficient exploration of the heuristic space using LLMs.
    *   Development of more robust and efficient adaptive selection mechanisms.
    *   Application of the HeurAgenix framework to a wider range of CO problems and real-world applications.

**Score: 9**

**Rationale:** HeurAgenix represents a significant advancement in LLM-based hyper-heuristics. Its novel approach to heuristic evolution and adaptive selection, coupled with its impressive empirical results, makes it a highly valuable contribution to the field. The framework addresses key limitations of previous approaches and offers a promising path towards more automated and generalizable optimization solutions. While the computational cost of the evolution phase and the limited LLM exploration represent minor weaknesses, the overall novelty, significance, and clarity of the work justify a high score. The paper is positioned to have a strong influence on future research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 9/10

### A Comparative Study of Task Adaptation Techniques of Large Language Models for Identifying Sustainable Development Goals
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15208v1)
- **Authors**: Andrea Cadeddu, Alessandro Chessa, Vincenzo De Leo, Gianni Fenu, Enrico Motta, Francesco Osborne, Diego Reforgiato Recupero, Angelo Salatino, Luca Secchi
- **Abstract**: In 2012, the United Nations introduced 17 Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) aimed at creating a more sustainable and improved future by 2030. However, tracking progress toward these goals is difficult because of the extensive scale and complexity of the data involved. Text classification models have become vital tools in this area, automating the analysis of vast amounts of text from a variety of sources. Additionally, large language models (LLMs) have recently proven indispensable for many natural language processing tasks, including text classification, thanks to their ability to recognize complex linguistic patterns and semantics. This study analyzes various proprietary and open-source LLMs for a single-label, multi-class text classification task focused on the SDGs. Then, it also evaluates the effectiveness of task adaptation techniques (i.e., in-context learning approaches), namely Zero-Shot and Few-Shot Learning, as well as Fine-Tuning within this domain. The results reveal that smaller models, when optimized through prompt engineering, can perform on par with larger models like OpenAI's GPT (Generative Pre-trained Transformer).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary**

The paper presents a comparative study of various task adaptation techniques applied to large language models (LLMs) for the specific task of identifying Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) from text. It examines several proprietary and open-source LLMs using Zero-Shot Learning, Few-Shot Learning (with different sampling strategies), and Fine-Tuning. A new benchmark dataset derived from the Open SDG (OSDG) initiative is used for evaluation. The study aims to determine if smaller, optimized LLMs can achieve comparable performance to larger models like OpenAI's GPT series.  The results show that fine-tuning generally yields the best performance, and that smaller models, when properly optimized through prompt engineering and fine-tuning on high-quality data, can compete with larger models. Quantization is also explored, demonstrating its effectiveness in low-resource settings. The authors also explore the influence of the selection of the few shot examples used in FSL by comparing a fully random approach to a semantic similarity based selection, demonstrating that the former perform better.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper contributes to the field by providing a new benchmark dataset for SDG text classification and systematically evaluating several LLMs (including recent ones like LLaMa 3 and Phi-3) using different task adaptation techniques.  While LLMs have been applied to text classification before, the specific focus on SDGs and the comprehensive comparison of various adaptation strategies and models is a valuable addition. The inclusion of different example selection method in Few Shot Learning (FSL) shows that example selection is an important hyperparameter to consider in the FSL approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant for several reasons:

    *   **Practical Implications:** It offers practical guidance for organizations aiming to leverage LLMs for SDG monitoring and reporting, especially those with limited resources. The paper shows that using smaller models can rival the results obtained using larger models.
    *   **Benchmarking:** The OSDG-derived benchmark dataset provides a valuable resource for future research in this area.
    *   **Model Comparison:** The detailed comparison of various LLMs and adaptation techniques allows for a more informed selection of models and strategies for SDG-related text classification tasks.
    *   **Highlighting example selection methods** The contribution of the FSL section highlights that example selection is an important hyperparameter to tune in the few shot learning approach.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation of a wide range of LLMs and task adaptation techniques.
    *   **Real-World Relevance:** The focus on SDGs makes the research highly relevant to current global challenges.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The experimental setup and evaluation metrics are clearly defined.
    *   **New Dataset:** The derived dataset allows to easily reproduce the experiments.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The propmts used in ZSL and FSL are made publicly available through a dedicated link.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Dataset Detail:** While the OSDG dataset is mentioned, a more detailed analysis of the dataset's characteristics (e.g., class distribution, data sources) would enhance the paper.
    *   **Prompt Engineering Details:** The prompts used for ZSL and FSL are crucial, but the paper could benefit from providing more specific examples and a more in-depth discussion of the prompt engineering process. While all the prompts used in ZSL and FSL are available online, only few examples are reported.
    *   **Computational Resources:** While the authors mention resource constraints, a more explicit discussion of the computational resources required for each experiment would be beneficial for readers seeking to replicate the findings.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in the application of LLMs for SDG monitoring and related areas. The benchmark dataset and comparative analysis will serve as a valuable reference point for researchers and practitioners. The insights into the effectiveness of different task adaptation techniques will also guide the development of more efficient and accurate SDG classification models.

*   **Justification:** The paper makes a solid contribution by creating a custom benchmark, and comparing the most performant LLMs in different settings (ZSL,FSL, and Fine Tuning) when approaching a single-label multi-class classification problems in the context of SDGs. Moreover, it showcases that prompting engineering can allow smaller models to rival the results of larger models.

**Score: 7**

*   **Rationale:** The paper is a solid piece of empirical research that provides practical guidance for using LLMs for SDG classification. The novelty lies in the specific application area (SDGs), the systematic evaluation of a wide range of models, and the creation of a benchmark dataset. While there are areas for improvement (e.g., more dataset detail, more examples of the prompts used), the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses.
    The influence potential influence on researchers and practitioners in the field is significant, warranting a score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### ProtoReasoning: Prototypes as the Foundation for Generalizable Reasoning in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15211v1)
- **Authors**: Feng He, Zijun Chen, Xinnian Liang, Tingting Ma, Yunqi Qiu, Shuangzhi Wu, Junchi Yan
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) trained with Long Chain-of-Thought (Long CoT) reasoning have demonstrated remarkable cross-domain generalization capabilities. However, the underlying mechanisms supporting such transfer remain poorly understood. We hypothesize that cross-domain generalization arises from shared abstract reasoning prototypes -- fundamental reasoning patterns that capture the essence of problems across domains.
